A vertical corner's chamfer\n162:   arriving at the plate is the case that is not fine: its chamfer lands tilted and lays\n164:   tells a corbel from a chamfer by reach, since a chamfer's reach is its size.\n166:   that has to slide onto something else. Lead-in chamfers at a mating mouth sound\n169:4. **Never polish a concave edge.** A cosmetic chamfer on an inside corner adds a thin\n172:   deliberate 3mm structural chamfer, sized for the load, before the polish pass runs.\n174:   is the one polish mistake that is invisible in code: the selector reads as an\n177:5. **Select chamfer edges by filtering, never blanket-chamfer.** Mating edges, back-face\n179:   **Filter for what must not be touched, then let the kernel refuse the rest.** A\n180:   chamfer call is all or nothing, so one edge that cannot land takes the whole pass\n182:   That is how a part ends up with three chamfered edges out of ninety. Narrow the set\n183:   for reasons you can name, then chamfer greedily: try the set, and where it fails,\n185:   than the corner triangle three chamfers leave, about `0.866 * size ** 2`, since\n186:   anything smaller is chamfers colliding. Chamfer the original solid with the whole\n188:   shape it was selected from, and applying batches in sequence quietly re-chamfers the\n197:7. **Consistency is the polish.** One chamfer size across a family is what makes it read\n200:**Sliver check after every polish pass.** Faces under 1mm² are a polish bug unless\n202:three 1mm chamfers meet at a convex corner, about 0.87mm². Anything else is usually\n203:chamfers colliding near a mating mouth, or a chamfered concave edge. A part declares the\n206:## Kernel rules\n210:- **Two chamfered convex edges need more than `2 * chamfer_size` of face between them.**\n212:  \"Failed creating a chamfer, try a smaller length value(s)\". At 1mm the shelf fails\n214:  The threshold tracks the chamfer exactly. This is the single most common way a part\n215:  stops building, so `chamfer` says this much at the point of failure rather than\n216:  leaving the kernel's advice to stand: taking \"try a smaller length\" at its word is how\n217:  a part ends up with a 0.4mm chamfer that lands and then prints as a defect.\n218:- **One chamfered edge needs more than `chamfer_size` of face**, which is the same rule\n219:  with one chamfer instead of two and is easy to miss because it looks like plenty of\n220:  room. It bites wherever a polished edge sits beside something that is never polished:\n221:  a concave junction, a structural chamfer's toe, a pocket wall. Measured on three\n223:  chamfer.\n224:- **A batch chamfer failure is not a bad edge.** Every edge in a failing set chamfers\n227:- **The second way a chamfer dies, where bisecting does not help**, is an edge whose end\n231:  nothing about the failure looks like a clearance problem. The fix is to chamfer in two\n235:- **Prefer `new_edges` over geometric selectors.** Each chamfer changes topology, so a\n236:  selector resolved against pristine geometry drifts once an earlier chamfer runs.\n242:  drop that existed only to escape `ASM_BL_NO_MATE` turned out to be the same \"a chamfer\n257:  lead-in chamfer that was retired on purpose.\n266:  its sliver count, its real minimum wall, its printer settings. The number goes next to\n276:min_wall = 1.0\n280:sliver = 6\n299:sliver = 26\n333:**A published number is a measurement, not a guess.** When the part mates with a manufactured product or a published standard, a VESA mount, a gridfinity bin, a camera thread, the number already exists in a datasheet, and making somebody caliper a hole pattern a standards body fixed is friction wearing rigor's clothes. Research it, record it like any other measurement with `how` naming the source (\"VESA MIS-D 100, manufacturer product page\"), and ask only for what nobody published, like the opening the finished thing has to fit. Two limits keep this honest: the user's actual object outranks the spec when the two disagree, because clones drift, and a number inferred from a product photo or a listing's marketing copy was never published at all, so it is a guess and gets marked like one.\n363:parts mount into: the machine, the wall, the shelf above. The joint angle is an\n367:**The declared range is a claim, and `nurb check` audits it** the way min_wall audits\n368:a wall: each hinge sweeps through it against everything else in the scene, and a\n382:collinear points make a razor sliver, and OCCT answers a boolean against a degenerate\n401:a kernel boolean cannot make. The sweep is the authority, and it is cheap.\n405:off assemblies entirely: each part already answered them alone, and overhang measured\n420:2. **Check fit-critical faces by coordinate** after any polish or resize. `nurb inspect`\n427:5. **Predict a baseline before you look at it.** Working out what the sliver count\n428:   should be from the polish exclusions turns the number into a test of the rule instead\n","exit_code":0,"command":"nurb rules 2>/dev/null | rg -n -i \"chamfer|kernel|polish|sliver|wall|sharp|hole|overhang|min_wall\" | head -120","truncated":false,"signal":null,"timed_out":false,"description":"Find chamfer, wall, and kernel rules","current_dir":"/private/var/folders/s4/by90nw7j7_l_nvvqs3hd9mjh0000gn/T/nurb-eval-trial-w5gxs8g_/project","output_file":"/var/folders/s4/by90nw7j7_l_nvvqs3hd9mjh0000gn/T/nurb-grok-home-gnauvad8/sessions/%2Fprivate%2Fvar%2Ffolders%2Fs4%2Fby90nw7j7_l_nvvqs3hd9mjh0000gn%2FT%2Fnurb-eval-trial-w5gxs8g_%2Fproject/01a03a44-e0cf-7d42-bc81-4ec8a4b4e99d/terminal/call-e44cbb31-4059-4e1f-8fe9-907a31ee8ed9-4.log","total_bytes":13912},"locations":[]}

{"type":"tool_call_update","toolCallId":"call-3dbd65c1-a190-46cf-9fd0-3853be9579c3-11","status":"completed","content":[{"type":"content","content":{"type":"text","text":"1→\"\"\"The bit_block task: a bench block for driver bits, dense with chamfers.\n\nA spec task like cable_clip, but aimed at the two abilities the first corpus never\ntouched. First, chamfer execution: every pocket mouth and the top perimeter get an\nexact 0.8 lead-in, with 2.0 webs between pockets, so the part sits close to OCCT's\nadjacent-chamfer limit and an agent that chamfers in the wrong order, or against\nselectors resolved before the pockets were cut, does not build at all. Second, count\nparametrization: the pocket grid is driven by an int parameter, and the flex probes\nregenerate the grid at other counts, which catches a grid written out by hand.\n10→\nEvery scored dimension is stated in the instruction, and nothing unstated is graded.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport math\nimport pathlib\nimport random\nimport shutil\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\n\n20→from build123d import Cylinder, GeomType, Pos, Vector\n\nfrom nurb import checks\n\nEPS = 1e-3\nTOL = 0.05  # stated dimensions are exact; this absorbs kernel noise, not design slack\n\nROWS = 2\nWEB = 2.0  # material between pocket walls, and from outer pockets to the block's side\nDEPTH = 12.0\n30→FLOOR = 3.0\nCHAM = 0.8\nCLEAR = 0.3\n\nINSTRUCTION = \"\"\"\\\nDesign a bench block that holds driver bits upright, and save it as\nparts/bit_block.py.\n\nThe bits' shanks are measured at {shank} mm across; the measurement is on file as\nshank_diameter in measurements.toml. Bits drop straight down into round pockets and\n40→stand there; the block sits flat on the bench.\n\nRequirements, all in mm:\n- A grid of {count} round pockets, {cols} columns by 2 rows, opening straight up.\n  Pocket diameter exactly shank_diameter + 0.3 = {pocket_d}, pocket depth exactly\n  12.0, flat floors, nothing intruding into any pocket and nothing roofing them over.\n- Grid pitch exactly {pitch} in both directions (2.0 of material between neighbouring\n  pocket walls), and 2.0 of material from the outermost pocket walls to the block's\n  sides, so the block is exactly {bbox_x} x {bbox_y}.\n- The floor under the pockets is solid, exactly 3.0 thick: overall height exactly\n50→  15.0.\n- Every pocket mouth gets an exact 0.8 x 45 degree chamfer lead-in. The top outer\n  perimeter gets the same 0.8 chamfer. No other edges are broken: the bottom\n  perimeter stays sharp so the stated bounding box is exact.\n- The part prints as it sits: flat bottom on the bed, one solid.\n- No material beyond what the features above require; the grader checks total volume\n  within 10% of nominal.\n- Expose shank_diameter as a float parameter and columns as an int parameter\n  (default {cols}), and derive the geometry from both: the block must rebuild\n  correctly for nearby shank sizes and other column counts.\n60→- nurb check must report zero findings. The grader runs the checks itself and\n  ignores the card's [accepted] blocks, so fix findings in the geometry instead of\n  accepting them.\n\"\"\"\n\nMEASUREMENTS = \"\"\"\\\n[shank_diameter]\nvalue = {shank}\nunit = \"mm\"\nhow = \"calipers across a bit shank, 2026-08-20\"\n70→\"\"\"\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass Instance:\n    seed: int\n    dims: dict\n    instruction: str\n    measurements: str\n\n80→\ndef _dims(shank, cols):\n    pocket_d = round(shank + CLEAR, 2)\n    pitch = round(pocket_d + WEB, 2)\n    return {\n        \"shank\": shank,\n        \"pocket_d\": pocket_d,\n        \"cols\": cols,\n        \"count\": cols * ROWS,\n        \"pitch\": pitch,\n90→        \"bbox_x\": round(cols * pitch + WEB, 2),\n        \"bbox_y\": round(ROWS * pitch + WEB, 2),\n        \"bbox_z\": round(FLOOR + DEPTH, 2),\n    }\n\n\ndef instance(seed):\n    rng = random.Random(seed)\n    shank = 4.0 + 0.25 * rng.randrange(17)\n    cols = 4 + rng.randrange(3)\n100→    dims = _dims(shank, cols)\n    return Instance(\n        seed=seed,\n        dims=dims,\n        instruction=INSTRUCTION.format(**dims),\n        measurements=MEASUREMENTS.format(shank=dims[\"shank\"]),\n    )\n\n\ndef context():\n110→    \"\"\"The Context this task is graded under. Frozen here, never read from the\n    candidate's card or printer.toml: a card's [accepted] block must not mute rules.\"\"\"\n    return checks.Context()\n\n\ndef _volume(dims):\n    r = dims[\"pocket_d\"] / 2\n    n = dims[\"count\"]\n    block = dims[\"bbox_x\"] * dims[\"bbox_y\"] * dims[\"bbox_z\"]\n    pockets = n * math.pi * r**2 * DEPTH\n120→    # The mouth chamfer ring, beyond the cylinder already subtracted.\n    rings = n * math.pi * CHAM**2 * (r + CHAM / 3)\n    # The perimeter chamfer prism, corners counted once.\n    perimeter = CHAM**2 / 2 * 2 * (dims[\"bbox_x\"] + dims[\"bbox_y\"]) - 4 * CHAM**3 / 3\n    return block - pockets - rings - perimeter\n\n\ndef _grid_centers(dims, bb):\n    \"\"\"Where the stated grid puts each pocket axis, from the bounding box alone.\"\"\"\n    r = dims[\"pocket_d\"] / 2\n130→    return [\n        (\n            bb.min.X + WEB + r + col * dims[\"pitch\"],\n            bb.min.Y + WEB + r + row * dims[\"pitch\"],\n        )\n        for col in range(dims[\"cols\"])\n        for row in range(ROWS)\n    ]\n\n\n140→def misfits(shape, dims):\n    \"\"\"Everything wrong with the block, as (problems, checks). Second, count\nparametrization: the pocket grid is driven by an int parameter, and the flex probes\nregenerate the grid at other counts, which catches a grid written out by hand.\n10→\nEvery scored dimension is stated in the instruction, and nothing unstated is graded.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport math\nimport pathlib\nimport random\nimport shutil\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\n\n20→from build123d import Cylinder, GeomType, Pos, Vector\n\nfrom nurb import checks\n\nEPS = 1e-3\nTOL = 0.05  # stated dimensions are exact; this absorbs kernel noise, not design slack\n\nROWS = 2\nWEB = 2.0  # material between pocket walls, and from outer pockets to the block's side\nDEPTH = 12.0\n30→FLOOR = 3.0\nCHAM = 0.8\nCLEAR = 0.3\n\nINSTRUCTION = \"\"\"\\\nDesign a bench block that holds driver bits upright, and save it as\nparts/bit_block.py.\n\nThe bits' shanks are measured at {shank} mm across; the measurement is on file as\nshank_diameter in measurements.toml. Bits drop straight down into round pockets and\n40→stand there; the block sits flat on the bench.\n\nRequirements, all in mm:\n- A grid of {count} round pockets, {cols} columns by 2 rows, opening straight up.\n  Pocket diameter exactly shank_diameter + 0.3 = {pocket_d}, pocket depth exactly\n  12.0, flat floors, nothing intruding into any pocket and nothing roofing them over.\n- Grid pitch exactly {pitch} in both directions (2.0 of material between neighbouring\n  pocket walls), and 2.0 of material from the outermost pocket walls to the block's\n  sides, so the block is exactly {bbox_x} x {bbox_y}.\n- The floor under the pockets is solid, exactly 3.0 thick: overall height exactly\n50→  15.0.\n- Every pocket mouth gets an exact 0.8 x 45 degree chamfer lead-in. The top outer\n  perimeter gets the same 0.8 chamfer. No other edges are broken: the bottom\n  perimeter stays sharp so the stated bounding box is exact.\n- The part prints as it sits: flat bottom on the bed, one solid.\n- No material beyond what the features above require; the grader checks total volume\n  within 10% of nominal.\n- Expose shank_diameter as a float parameter and columns as an int parameter\n  (default {cols}), and derive the geometry from both: the block must rebuild\n  correctly for nearby shank sizes and other column counts.\n60→- nurb check must report zero findings. The grader runs the checks itself and\n  ignores the card's [accepted] blocks, so fix findings in the geometry instead of\n  accepting them.\n\"\"\"\n\nMEASUREMENTS = \"\"\"\\\n[shank_diameter]\nvalue = {shank}\nunit = \"mm\"\nhow = \"calipers across a bit shank, 2026-08-20\"\n70→\"\"\"\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass Instance:\n    seed: int\n    dims: dict\n    instruction: str\n    measurements: str\n\n80→\ndef _dims(shank, cols):\n    pocket_d = round(shank + CLEAR, 2)\n    pitch = round(pocket_d + WEB, 2)\n    return {\n        \"shank\": shank,\n        \"pocket_d\": pocket_d,\n        \"cols\": cols,\n        \"count\": cols * ROWS,\n        \"pitch\": pitch,\n90→        \"bbox_x\": round(cols * pitch + WEB, 2),\n        \"bbox_y\": round(ROWS * pitch + WEB, 2),\n        \"bbox_z\": round(FLOOR + DEPTH, 2),\n    }\n\n\ndef instance(seed):\n    rng = random.Random(seed)\n    shank = 4.0 + 0.25 * rng.randrange(17)\n    cols = 4 + rng.randrange(3)\n100→    dims = _dims(shank, cols)\n    return Instance(\n        seed=seed,\n        dims=dims,\n        instruction=INSTRUCTION.format(**dims),\n        measurements=MEASUREMENTS.format(shank=dims[\"shank\"]),\n    )\n\n\ndef context():\n110→    \"\"\"The Context this task is graded under. Frozen here, never read from the\n    candidate's card or printer.toml: a card's [accepted] block must not mute rules.\"\"\"\n    return checks.Context()\n\n\ndef _volume(dims):\n    r = dims[\"pocket_d\"] / 2\n    n = dims[\"count\"]\n    block = dims[\"bbox_x\"] * dims[\"bbox_y\"] * dims[\"bbox_z\"]\n    pockets = n * math.pi * r**2 * DEPTH\n120→    # The mouth chamfer ring, beyond the cylinder already subtracted.\n    rings = n * math.pi * CHAM**2 * (r + CHAM / 3)\n    # The perimeter chamfer prism, corners counted once.\n    perimeter = CHAM**2 / 2 * 2 * (dims[\"bbox_x\"] + dims[\"bbox_y\"]) - 4 * CHAM**3 / 3\n    return block - pockets - rings - perimeter\n\n\ndef _grid_centers(dims, bb):\n    \"\"\"Where the stated grid puts each pocket axis, from the bounding box alone.\"\"\"\n    r = dims[\"pocket_d\"] / 2\n130→    return [\n        (\n            bb.min.X + WEB + r + col * dims[\"pitch\"],\n            bb.min.Y + WEB + r + row * dims[\"pitch\"],\n        )\n        for col in range(dims[\"cols\"])\n        for row in range(ROWS)\n    ]\n\n\n140→def misfits(shape, dims):\n    \"\"\"Everything wrong with the block, as (problems, checks). Translation-tolerant,\n    rotation-pinned: the grid is derived from the bounding box, which the stated\n    borders pin to the pockets, so every per-pocket check is against stated geometry\n    rather than whatever centers the candidate chose.\"\"\"\n    problems = []\n    checks_run = 0\n    bb = shape.bounding_box()\n    r = dims[\"pocket_d\"] / 2\n\n150→    for axis, want in ((\"X\", dims[\"bbox_x\"]), (\"Y\", dims[\"bbox_y\"]), (\"Z\", dims[\"bbox_z\"])):\n        checks_run += 1\n        got = getattr(bb.size, axis)\n        if abs(got - want) > TOL:\n            problems.append(f\"bounding box {axis} is {got:.2f}mm, expected {want}\")\n\n    centers = _grid_centers(dims, bb)\n\n    # The bottom: one flat rectangular face spanning the full footprint. TOL, bb.max.Z + 4.0 - floor_z\n        )\n        try:\n            hit = shape & probe\n            if (hit.volume if hit is not None else 0.0) > 0.01:\n260→                blocked += 1\n        except Exception:\n            blocked += 1\n    if blocked:\n        problems.append(f\"{blocked} of {len(centers)} pockets are not clear for a straight drop-in\")\n\n    # Solid under the floors and between the pockets: the stated material, probed\n    # where neither chamfer can reach.\n    checks_run += 1\n    web_x = bb.min.X + WEB + r + dims[\"pitch\"] / 2  # between the first two columns\n270→    web_y = (bb.min.Y + bb.max.Y) / 2\n    solid = all(\n        shape.is_inside(Vector(cx, cy, bb.min.Z + FLOOR / 2)) for cx, cy in centers\n    ) and shape.is_inside(Vector(web_x, web_y, bb.max.Z - want) > 0.10 * want:\n        problems.append(f\"volume {shape.volume:.0f}mm3 is off nominal {want:.0f}mm3 by >10%\")\n\n    return problems, checks_run\n\n\ndef _matched(centers, edges):\n    \"\"\"How many stated grid centers have one of `edges` on their axis.\"\"\"\n    count = 0\n    for cx, cy in centers:\n        if any(\n            abs(e.arc_center.X - cx) < TOL and abs(e.arc_center.Y - cy) < TOL for e in edges\n        ):\n            count += 1\n    return count\n\n\ndef flex_probes(inst):\n    \"\"\"Parameter overrides and matching ground truth for the isolated build worker.\n    The column probes are what catch a grid written out by hand instead of derived\n    from the int parameter.\"\"\"\n    shank = inst.dims[\"shank\"]\n    cols = inst.dims[\"cols\"]\n    return [\n        ({\"params\": {\"shank_diameter\": round(shank + 0.5, 2)}}, _dims(round(shank + 0.5, 2), cols)),\n        ({\"params\": {\"columns\": cols + 1}}, _dims(shank, cols + 1)),\n        (\n            {\"params\": {\"shank_diameter\": round(shank + 1.0, 2), \"columns\": cols - 1}},\n            _dims(round(shank + 1.0, 2), cols - 1),\n        ),\n    ]\n\n\ndef materialize(seed, dest):\n    \"\"\"Write the project a model starts from: fixture, the seeded measurement, and the\n    same AGENTS.md a real project gets from `nurb new`, so the model designs with the\n    shipped skill in front of it exactly the way a user's session would.\"\"\"\n    import importlib.resources\n\n    dest = pathlib.Path(dest)\n    fixture = pathlib.Path(__file__).parent / \"fixture\"\n    shutil.copytree(fixture, dest, dirs_exist_ok=True)\n    (dest / \"measurements.toml\").write_text(instance(seed).measurements, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    skill = importlib.resources.files(\"nurb\").joinpath(\"agents.md\").read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    (dest / \"AGENTS.md\").write_text(skill, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    return dest\n","total_lines":325}},"locations":[]}

{"type":"tool_call_update","toolCallId":"call-8fa5f2b5-4cb5-4c5d-92ad-950f44f8c2bd-14","status":"completed","content":[{"type":"content","content":{"type":"text","text":"1→\"\"\"The polish pass: chamfer as much of a set as the kernel will take.\n\n`chamfer` is all or nothing. One edge that cannot land takes the whole call down, and\nthe natural response is to shrink the set by hand until it builds. That is how a part\nwith ninety exposed edges ends up with three chamfered ones, and it is why the same\ndoctrine produces far more chamfers in a GUI, where a human adds them one feature at a\ntime and works around each collision individually.\n\nThe doctrine describes this algorithm and used to leave every project to write it. It\n10→was 35 lines in one `system.py`, which meant the next project would write it again or,\nfar more likely, skip it and hand-narrow the set, which is exactly what the rule exists\nto prevent.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport collections.abc\nimport functools\n\nfrom build123d import chamfer as _chamfer\n\n20→# What OCCT says when a chamfer will not land. The same strings `nurb verify`\n# matches on, because both are asking the same question: did the kernel refuse, or did\n# the part refuse in its own words.\nKERNEL = (\"Failed creating a chamfer\", \"BRep_API\")\n\n\ndef _advice(count, size, word):\n    \"\"\"What the doctrine knows about this failure, said at the moment it happens.\n\n    The kernel's own message is \"try a smaller length value(s)\", and the doctrine is\n30→    explicit that following it would never have found the real rule: a chamfer fails\n    for room, not for length. Shrinking it is the one response that makes a part quietly\n    worse, because a 0.4mm chamfer lands and then reads as a defect on the print.\n\n    So the rule goes here rather than only in `nurb rules`. This is the most common way\n    a part stops building, and an error is the one place a reader is guaranteed to be\n    looking.\n    \"\"\"\n    lines = [\n        \"\",\n40→        f\"  A {word} fails for room, not for length, and shrinking it is usually the\",\n        f\"  wrong fix. The brim is\n    the slicer's share and the message says so, but a brim on sharp corners is\n    treating the symptom.\n\n    Only faces that are themselves plate-sized get their corners read: the ribs\n    under a properly ribbed floor are skinny rectangles full of sharp corners, and\n    each pulls far too little to peel.\n    \"\"\"\n    up = Vector(*ctx.up).normalized()\n    bed, _ = _span(shape, up)\n    footing = [f for f in shape.faces() if _span(f, up)[1] <= bed + 1e-4]\n    area = sum(f.area for f in footing)\n    pull = SHRINK[(ctx.material or \"pla\").lower()] / SHRINK[\"pla\"]\n    if area * pull <= ctx.warp_area:\n        return []\n    sharp = [\n        point\n        for face in footing\n        if face.area * pull >= ctx.warp_area / 2\n        for point in _sharp_plan_corners(face, up, ctx.warp_radius)\n    ]\n    if not sharp:\n        return []\n    centre = sum((f.center() * f.area for f in footing), Vector(0, 0, 0)) / area\n    worst = max(sharp, key=lambda point: (point - centre).length)\n    count = len(sharp)\n    made_of = f\" in {ctx.material.upper()}, which contracts {pull:.0f}x what PLA does\" if pull > 1 else \"\"\n    plastic = ctx.material.upper() if pull > 1 else \"The plastic\"\n    return [\n        Finding(\n            \"warp_risk\",\n            WARN,\n            f\"{count} sharp corner{'' if count == 1 else 's'} on a {area:.0f}mm2 \"\n            f\"first layer{made_of}; contraction peels them as the print cools. Round \"\n            f\"plan corners past {ctx.warp_radius:.0f}mm, rib the floor instead of a \"\n            f\"solid slab, and print with a brim\",\n            plain=f\"a big flat base with sharp corners. {plastic} shrinks as it \"\n            \"cools and curls them off the bed; rounded corners and a brim keep it down\",\n","total_lines":1668}},"locations":[]}

Bits drop into round pockets; the\nblock sits flat on the bench.\n\n## What it is\n\nA solid rectangular block with a 5-by-2 grid of round pockets (columns and\nshank_diameter are parameters). Pocket diameter is shank_diameter + 0.3, depth\n12, with a 3 mm solid floor. Each pocket mouth and the top outer perimeter get\nan 0.8 x 45° chamfer. The bottom perimeter stays sharp so the part prints on\nits full footprint.\n\n## Design notes\n\n- Grid pitch is pocket diameter plus 2 mm of web, with 2 mm of web to each side.\n- Pocket mouths and the top perimeter are chamfered in one call on the top\n  face's edges, after the pockets are cut. The 2.0 mm web is only just above\n  OCCT's 2 × chamfer clearance; splitting the pass or selecting edges before\n  the pockets exist fails to build.\n- Bottom edges are left sharp on purpose: a bed chamfer would shrink the\n  bounding box.\n\n## Don't\n\n- Don't chamfer the bottom perimeter. The bounding box and bed face have to\n  stay the full footprint.\n- Don't chamfer pocket mouths and the top perimeter in two passes, and don't\n  resolve those edges before the pockets are cut.\n- Don't polish every exposed edge. That would break the bottom.\n\n## Changelog\n\n- Initial: 5×2 bit block, 0.8 lead-in on mouths and top rim, sharp bed.\n"},"content":[],"locations":[]}

Pocket diameter is shank_diameter + 0.3, depth\n12, with a 3 mm solid floor. Each pocket mouth and the top outer perimeter get\nan 0.8 x 45° chamfer. The bottom perimeter stays sharp so the part prints on\nits full footprint.\n\n## Design notes\n\n- Grid pitch is pocket diameter plus 2 mm of web, with 2 mm of web to each side.\n- Pocket mouths and the top perimeter are chamfered in one call on the top\n  face's edges, after the pockets are cut. The 2.0 mm web is only just above\n  OCCT's 2 × chamfer clearance; splitting the pass or selecting edges before\n  the pockets exist fails to build.\n- Bottom edges are left sharp on purpose: a bed chamfer would shrink the\n  bounding box.\n\n## Don't\n\n- Don't chamfer the bottom perimeter. The bounding box and bed face have to\n  stay the full footprint.\n- Don't chamfer pocket mouths and the top perimeter in two passes, and don't\n  resolve those edges before the pockets are cut.\n- Don't polish every exposed edge.
